There are logjams and if we want to make progress towards the total elimination of apartheid in this country, we must be prepared to understand the problems of <span class="col_6547-6548" refersTo="page_0280"/>the various constituencies and to understand the difficulties that each party and each side has.</p>

<p>Of course, in a parliamentary system if we want to place too much emphasis on the Westminster System, we must give recognition to the fact that our whole structure and system is dependent on constituency politics. The survival of significant groups in each House&#x2014;significant in bringing about further changes in this country&#x2014;is important to ensure that they are strengthened and therefore when we want to clear the logjam we must engage in an exercise of accommodating each other and understanding each other&#x2019;s difficulties. We must not be purists or behave like pundits. I really compliment the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition who stood up here this afternoon &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>He is a good leader.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Unlike the hon member for Stanger, who made apologies! The hon Leader of the Official Opposition did not make apologies because&#x2014;and I know this&#x2014;he does not adopt a purist attitude.</p>

<p>In spite of the fact that he mentioned that there are certain things in these Rules which he also does not like he was strong enough to stand up and be counted for progress&#x2014;progress in an evolutionary manner&#x2014;in this country.</p>

<p>The adoption of these Standing Rules and Orders by this House this afternoon means that we are taking a very important step forward together.</p>

<p>We have been hard and we have adopted strong attitudes and the same goes for the House of Representatives, but they adopted it unanimously.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>They had a division yesterday.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chief_whip_of_parliament">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHIEF WHIP OF PARLIAMENT</person>:</from>

<p>They did not have a division, but the Opposition objected.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The Opposition objected.</p>

<p>We are now going to be confronted with certain situations. Where one sits is not embodied in the Rules. What is an opposition? An opposition party is an opposition with regard to own affairs. We cannot sit in a joint chamber and say we are the official opposition parties. One cannot classify Solidarity with the CP because it is an official opposition party in a different House. If an opposition party opposes the Government it does so for various reasons eg the PFP opposed the reform programme of the Government in the referendum.</p>

<p>The conservative element opposed the Government and their supporters recorded the noes. If there were 10 000 noes, they could be for different reasons. One group opposed the Government because they are changing the country and another group is opposing the Government because they are not changing fast enough.</p>

<p>Therefore we now have to address another problem in joint debates and this will probably be the issue with which the Rules Committee will be dealing with from time to time. When we arrived here in 1984 we did not foresee that we were going to debate jointly. As I have stated, the tradition of Parhament in its first week is not to try to topple the Government, but to afford the oppositions in each House the opportunity to express a view. The country will be looking forward to an important factor in the first week after the official opening of Parliament, namely the discussion of the state of the nation.</p>

<p>Therefore I think that the former PRP element has really exposed itself seriously today in that these people appear to have forgotten what they did in September last year. The argument that they are now PFP members cannot hold, because at that time they were actually carted around by the PFP. We know what the PFP did in that Chamber, they did here.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>What does it matter? You joined the party!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>We are not associated with the NP.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Of course you are.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I suggest that the hon member for Springfield, because of the humiliation to which he was subjected today, has forgotten that there is such a thing as a September 1987 Hansard.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>We know about your caucus. The Ministers were brought in there and you sell your party down the river.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member has <span class="col_6549-6550" refersTo="page_0281"/>referred to Ministers coming in. Can the hon member for Springfield deny the fact that, when he was with Solidarity, a general affairs Minister attended and addressed the caucus of Solidarity?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Of course that is true.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Right, but if that is true, how can it be bad for others?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>We are not selling the community down the river like you are.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>No, we never had a Minister in our caucus.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member for Springfield admitted that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>I beg your pardon? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, we had meetings elsewhere but not a meeting with our caucus. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Whatever it is, I do not want to cross swords with the hon member for Stanger, but there is absolutely no harm in that. Therefore if it is not wrong with Solidarity and with the former PRP members conceding that, let us not split hairs about this.</p>

<p>If we want to talk about reforms in this country, the Nationalist Party is leaving the PFP lengths behind as far as honesty in changes is concerned.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>At the end of my speech, Mr Chairman. Now I would like to ask the hon member for Springfield a question. In talking about promoting apartheid, there are certain levels of Government within which his party members are in the majority. They should not go and hide behind the Government. They should stand up at the level of local government and reject the apartheid which they are practising.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>That is nonsense and you know it!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Let them reject it. There are many instances; for instance apartheid in municipal budgets. Show me any Central Government legislation which states that you must discriminate in respect of the allocation of funds.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Since when is that PFP policy? Nonsense!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That is PFP policy because the PFP members practise apartheid. Most of the humiliation which Indian people have been subjected to in urban areas was at the hands of PFP members.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Fiddlesticks! Rubbish! Hogwash!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>When it suits them, they hide behind Government policy.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Hogwash!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council whether he can deny that the Nationalist Government of this country is now advocating and implementing what members of the PFP were advocating 20 years ago? In other words, can he deny that the National Peoples Party and the Nationalist Government of this country are 20 years behind the PFP? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I am aware of a famous Prog saying 20 years ago: &#x201C;Don&#x2019;t give the Indians the vote; give them the boat.&#x201D;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>That is nonsense! Which member of the PFP was that? Who was he? Name him!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>However, it was the NP that said: &#x201C;No longer give the Indians the boat; give them the vote.&#x201D; That is the answer.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Who said that?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You know who it was.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Rubbish!</p>

<p>Question agreed to (Progressive Federal Party dissenting).</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#adjournment_of_house">

<heading>ADJOURNMENT OF HOUSE</heading>

<summary>(Motion)</summary>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I move:</p>

<block name="quote">That the House do now adjourn.</block>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</speech>

<adjournment>

<p>The House adjourned at <recordedTime time="1988-04-19T17:51:00">17h51.</recordedTime></p>

</adjournment>

</debateSection>

</debateSection>

</debateSection>
